self = <psycopg_pool.pool_async.AsyncConnectionPool 'pool-1' at 0x128f63bcd70>
timeout = 30.0

    async def getconn(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> ACT:
        """Obtain a connection from the pool.
    
        You should preferably use `connection()`. Use this function only if
        it is not possible to use the connection as context manager.
    
        After using this function you *must* call a corresponding `putconn()`:
        failing to do so will deplete the pool. A depleted pool is a sad pool:
        you don't want a depleted pool.
        """
        if timeout is None:
            timeout = self.timeout
        deadline = monotonic() + timeout
    
        logger.info("connection requested from %r", self.name)
        self._stats[self._REQUESTS_NUM] += 1
    
        self._check_open_getconn()
    
        try:
            return await self._getconn_with_check_loop(deadline)
    
        # Re-raise the timeout exception presenting the user the global
        # timeout, not the per-attempt one.
        except PoolTimeout:
>           raise PoolTimeout(
                f"couldn't get a connection after {timeout:.2f} sec"
            ) from None

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None

                last = exc

                continue

                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")

                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

self = <psycopg_pool.pool_async.AsyncConnectionPool 'pool-2' at 0x128ff4bf110>
timeout = 30.0

    async def getconn(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> ACT:
        """Obtain a connection from the pool.
    
        You should preferably use `connection()`. Use this function only if
        it is not possible to use the connection as context manager.
    
        After using this function you *must* call a corresponding `putconn()`:
        failing to do so will deplete the pool. A depleted pool is a sad pool:
        you don't want a depleted pool.
        """
        if timeout is None:
            timeout = self.timeout
        deadline = monotonic() + timeout
    
        logger.info("connection requested from %r", self.name)
        self._stats[self._REQUESTS_NUM] += 1
    
        self._check_open_getconn()
    
        try:
            return await self._getconn_with_check_loop(deadline)
    
        # Re-raise the timeout exception presenting the user the global
        # timeout, not the per-attempt one.
        except PoolTimeout:
>           raise PoolTimeout(
                f"couldn't get a connection after {timeout:.2f} sec"
            ) from None
E           psycopg_pool.PoolTimeout: couldn't get a connection after 30.00 sec

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

engines = (<kx.crm.pdf.WeasyPrintEngine object at 0x00000128813D1910>,)
company = ''

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None

            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])

            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None

                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None

                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""
        last: Exception | None = None
        for i, engine in enumerate(engines):
            try:
                data = await engine.render(html)
            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])
                continue
            if i > 0:
                emit_metric("VendorSwitch", 1, company=company or "unknown", field="pdf", vendor=engine.name)
            return data

    async def render_pdf(html: str, engines: Sequence[PdfEngine], company: str = "") -> bytes:
        """First engine that works wins (D37: Chromium -> WeasyPrint). A switch is logged and counted."""

            except Exception as exc:
                last = exc
                log.warning("pdf.engine_failed", engine=engine.name, error=str(exc)[:300])

            return data

name = 'libgobject-2.0-0', flags = 4096

    def _load_backend_lib(backend, name, flags):
        import os
        if not isinstance(name, basestring):
            if sys.platform != "win32" or name is not None:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            name = "c"    # Windows: load_library(None) fails, but this works
                          # on Python 2 (backward compatibility hack only)
        first_error = None
        if '.' in name or '/' in name or os.sep in name:
            try:
                return backend.load_library(name, flags)
            except OSError as e:
                first_error = e
        import ctypes.util
        path = ctypes.util.find_library(name)
        if path is None:
            if name == "c" and sys.platform == "win32" and sys.version_info >= (3,):
                raise OSError("dlopen(None) cannot work on Windows for Python 3 "
                              "(see http://bugs.python.org/issue23606)")
            msg = ("ctypes.util.find_library() did not manage "
                   "to locate a library called %r" % (name,))
            if first_error is not None:
                msg = "%s.  Additionally, %s" % (first_error, msg)
            raise OSError(msg)
>       return backend.load_library(path, flags)
